# Game Design Transformation Audit

This audit tracks the repository-level conversion from the legacy web/app UI generator identity into AI Game Design Studio as a game-design operating system. It is intentionally evidence-based: a requirement is not considered complete unless there is a concrete file, test, verifier, or runtime surface that demonstrates it.

For the item-by-item prompt coverage table, see [`game-design-requirement-matrix.md`](game-design-requirement-matrix.md). That matrix maps all 100 numbered requirements to current evidence and explicitly labels partial runtime ambitions. For the final proof pass, use [`game-design-completion-audit.md`](game-design-completion-audit.md), which restates the objective as deliverables and maps prompt requirements to concrete artifacts and commands. Compatibility-only legacy names are governed by [`game-design-compatibility-manifest.md`](game-design-compatibility-manifest.md).

## Objective

Transform the repository so prompts, agents, workflows, schemas, templates, docs, components, UI copy, exports, and evaluation systems are game-native. The platform should generate playable concepts, game systems, worldbuilding packages, HUDs, level boards, economy/progression specs, narrative systems, multiplayer planning, art/audio direction, and production-ready game documentation instead of generic product screens.

## Success Criteria

1. Studio identity reads as an AI game design studio in README, quickstart, contribution docs, package metadata, public site, and in-app labels.
2. Prompt stack rejects non-game builder behavior and reasons in terms of genre, gameplay loop, player experience, game feel, level flow, balancing, accessibility, and production feasibility.
3. Skill catalog contains game-native skills only; retired app/web/business skill ids no longer redirect into active generation modes or show as first-class options.
4. Game art bibles replace generic design-system guidance in user-facing copy while preserving `DESIGN.md` compatibility where the runtime still requires the file format.
5. Schemas and persistence support game-domain entities, memory consistency, game documents, scene viewports, node graphs, behavior trees, and system specs.
6. Templates and prompt templates prioritize game deliverables: playable scenes, HUDs, GDDs, level maps, combat specs, key art, trailers, audio kits, live-ops boards, and balance sheets.
7. Frontend surfaces feel like a studio workspace: game project creation, game art bible picker, game skills, viewport/node/behavior-system document editor, and game-specific examples.
8. Evaluation, the compatibility manifest, and guardrails detect non-game regressions, legacy prompt templates, non-game skills, and artifacts that drift into app/product surfaces.
9. Documentation teaches game design production: gameplay loops, combat, level design, HUD readability, progression, economy, multiplayer, procedural generation, accessibility, camera, game feel, and art direction.
10. Verification covers the above with targeted tests, typechecks, guard checks, and explicit residual-risk notes.

## Known Gaps

- Some internal names still use `designSystemId`, `.agds/design-systems`, `OD_*`, and `.od` for compatibility with existing project metadata, stored wrappers, runtime installs, and `DESIGN.md` loading; canonical `/api/game-art-bibles`, `game-art-bibles/`, `GameArtBible*`, app-config `gameArtBibleId`, MCP `agds://game-art-bibles`, `AGDS_*`, and `agds` names are now available. Shared registry `DesignSystem*` response aliases, `/api/design-systems` daemon routes, packaged `od://` aliases, and MCP `od://` resource aliases have been removed, `docs/game-design-compatibility-manifest.md` defines the allowed compatibility surfaces, and `scripts/guard.ts` now confines the remaining art-bible and OD runtime identifiers to explicit migration/wrapper modules.
- Critique Theater still accepts deprecated panelist roles (`designer`, `critic`, `brand`, `a11y`, `copy`) for historical transcripts, but rendered prompts, default runtime cast, current parser/orchestrator fixtures, transcript helpers, and spawn-path synthetic streams use game-studio roles plus the canonical `GAME_ART_BIBLE_SOURCE` reference wrapper. `scripts/guard.ts` now confines deprecated role strings to the shared compatibility list, legacy weights, one explicit parser compatibility test, and prompt tests that assert old role blocks are absent.
- Negative/compatibility docs intentionally mention forbidden product surfaces to explain what must not be generated.
- Non-English locales now have automated checks for canonical product identity plus obvious translated design-system/app/site leaks in art-bible catalog, deploy, notification, packaged-studio, connector, onboarding, MCP setup/install copy, React gameplay-module file metadata, and Orbit copy. First-party connector category locale keys now use game/studio nouns such as `playerCommunity` and `productionFinance` while raw external categories such as CRM/commerce are only classifier inputs; connector discipline labels, connector tool pagination controls, visible tool action labels, pet slash-command help, short studio status labels, preview unavailable messages, reviewed connector category/panel catalogs for Indonesian/German/Spanish/Japanese, reviewed New Project front-door controls for Indonesian/German/Spanish/Japanese/French, New Project tab-arrow/beta/editor-surface labels game-brief fields, New Project live-ops controls, residual New Project front-door labels, New Project game-media and connector guidance, Cloudflare game artifact deploy controls, live artifact picker/editor/OAuth controls, model-discovery settings, Game Projects live status badges, the canonical studio preview pill, core studio workspace labels, workspace focus/chat toggles, Game Files labels and workspace cross-links, Studio Mode Strip labels/descriptions, scheduled studio runs controls/history/status copy, studio document editor chrome, Game Telemetry Board labels/empty states, product identity strings, and example discipline filters now have exact expected values or targeted forbidden-term checks instead of broad English fallbacks. `pnpm i18n:fallback-audit` now fails if any exact-English fallback outside the technical/proper-noun allowlist returns; on May 15, 2026 it found `0` exact English values beyond a conservative technical/proper-noun allowlist in every non-English dictionary. - The same P0 game-design artifact gate now covers ingress paths beyond manual project saves. Imported folders are scanned before project creation, ZIP imports validate decoded files before creating a project directory, chat attachment uploads run the lint before responding and delete rejected uploads, and focused tests prove legacy markdown/HTML packages are rejected from those paths. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run tests/game-studio-file-validation.test.ts tests/folder-import-route.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`26` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-studio-import.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`8` tests), and daemon typecheck.

- The main guard now locks project artifact lint enforcement so future changes cannot remove P0 game-design lint from static artifact save, project save, rename, upload, folder-import, ZIP-import, final game-package synthesis, live-artifact preview persistence, or Critique Theater SHIP artifact paths without failing `pnpm guard`. Validation passed with `pnpm exec tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.json --noEmit` and `pnpm guard`.

- Runtime artifact evaluation now recognizes the same advanced game-system vocabulary instead of stopping at broad studio-quality axes. `GAME_EVALUATION_AXES` adds deterministic coverage for quest/mission structure, encounter design, open-world simulation, survival pressure, stealth fairness, traversal/vehicles, camera/animation/VFX, lighting/atmosphere, audio direction, asset pipelines, telemetry/playtesting, and genre benchmarking; daemon lint tests prove those axes prevent advanced Markdown specs from being treated as thin game artifacts.

- Model-facing discovery onboarding now explicitly asks the required game-studio brief fields rather than relying on a catch-all constraints box. The turn-1 `<question-form id="discovery">` covers genre/player fantasy, single-player vs multiplayer, mobile/PC/console/web/Steam Deck/cloud targets, art style, monetization/release model, story/systems/competitive/casual emphasis, 2D/3D camera, session length, audience age, emotional goal, controls, engine, and gameplay inspirations. Daemon prompt tests and the built contracts package-runtime test pin those fields.

- Frontend New Project onboarding now offers the same broader platform surface, including console, Steam Deck, and cloud gaming, and its focused component test now proves genre, player mode, platform, dimensionality, camera, engine, input, session length, audience, emotional goal, monetization, art style, inspirations, and editor-surface choices persist into `metadata.gameDesign`.

- Agent-facing prompt metadata now preserves that full onboarding payload under `### Game design memory`, so the captured game brief is not only stored in project metadata but also visible to the generation prompt on future turns. The focused daemon prompt test pins every core onboarding field plus editor surfaces.

- Durable game-memory schemas now cover advanced studio systems beyond the original required entity list. `GAME_ENTITY_TYPES` accepts survival systems, stealth systems, vehicle systems, live-ops plans, encounter specs, procedural rules, animation/VFX/lighting/audio systems, asset pipelines, open-world regions, telemetry models, genre benchmarks, playtest simulations, community systems, modding pipelines, difficulty directors, companions, and related production docs. Verification passed with the contracts game-studio test, package-runtime import test, contracts build/typecheck, daemon prompt test, and daemon typecheck under Node 24.

- The core model-facing memory charter now names every durable `GAME_ENTITY_TYPES` id as first-class studio memory, including live-ops plans, encounters, procedural rules, behavior trees, dynamic events, camera/animation/VFX/lighting/audio systems, asset pipelines, character/quest systems, open-world regions, survival, stealth, vehicles, telemetry, feasibility, benchmarks, scaling variants, playtest simulations, node/viewport documents, accessibility and engine profiles, boss/economy/narrative/faction/level docs, community/modding systems, difficulty directors, and companions. - Studio-document collaboration now has a CRDT-style operation merge route instead of only whole-file saves and presence refresh. Shared contracts in `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` define JSON document operations, operation requests/responses, operation results, and `studio_document_operations` SSE payloads. `apps/daemon/src/studio-document-operations.ts` normalizes bounded JSON operations, sorts concurrent edits by lamport/actor/id, applies `json-set`, `json-delete`, and idempotent `json-array-append` operations, persists an operation log under `.agds/studio-document-operations`, suppresses duplicate operation ids or duplicate array item identities, increments document revisions only for applied operations, and returns the merged content. `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `POST /api/projects/:id/studio-document-operations` and `/api/game-deliverables/:id/studio-document-operations`, writes the merged JSON through normal game-studio document validation, updates the project timestamp, and emits revision SSE. `apps/web/src/providers/registry.ts` now posts browser-originated studio document operations, `apps/web/src/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.tsx` submits changed top-level JSON sections as `json-set`/`json-delete` operations with whole-file fallback, emits selection/source cursor metadata, and renders matching remote cursor chips inside the document surface. `apps/web/src/components/FileWorkspace.tsx` forwards local cursors through workspace presence and filters remote presence down to the active file, `apps/web/src/providers/project-events.ts` parses revision events, and `apps/web/src/components/ProjectView.tsx` treats applied operation events as studio-document freshness signals while posting cursor-aware studio presence. `apps/daemon/tests/studio-document-operations-routes.test.ts` proves concurrent node-graph operations merge deterministically, duplicate operations are skipped idempotently, revision counters advance only for applied operations, and the merged node graph remains valid. Web provider/component tests prove the operation endpoint client, editor operation-save path, SSE payload parsing, open-workspace refresh behavior, cursor metadata propagation, and in-editor remote cursor rendering. The requirement matrix now treats operation-level CRDT-style merging, browser operation submission, web collaborator refresh, cursor-aware presence, and active-document remote cursor visibility as implemented while still leaving a browser-embedded multi-cursor CRDT collaborative editor partial. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx tests/components/FileWorkspace.test.tsx --testTimeout 30000` (`41` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx tests/providers/registry.test.ts --testTimeout 30000` (`41` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/providers/project-events.test.ts tests/components/ProjectView.pendingPrompt.test.tsx --testTimeout 30000` (`19` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts typecheck`, and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts tests/public-docs.test.ts tests/spec-docs.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`5` tests).

- The audit now has a strict companion coverage table in `docs/game-design-requirement-matrix.md`. The new matrix maps all 100 numbered transformation requirements to concrete repository evidence and labels partial runtime ambitions instead of treating broad guard/test success as proof. It now keeps native-language review explicit while routing intentional compatibility aliases through `docs/game-design-compatibility-manifest.md`, and it treats external agent scheduling, spatial editing, telemetry ingestion, playtest/runtime simulation, autonomous iteration, and studio-document co-editing as implemented where the repository has concrete routes, UI, or tests. `apps/daemon/tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts` proves the matrix contains exactly rows 1 through 100, keeps key partial/non-completion statuses explicit, and remains linked from this audit. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts tests/public-docs.test.ts tests/spec-docs.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`5` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json --noEmit`, `pnpm guard`, `git diff --check`, and a focused matrix scan for old-product wording.

- The `.gameview.json` editor now has a first real spatial-editing affordance beyond JSON source editing. In `GameStudioDocumentEditor`, viewport documents support adding a player-facing `interaction-zone` marker, selecting the new marker, moving selected entities with inspector nudge controls, moving selected entities with arrow-key nudges on the viewport canvas, and saving the updated coordinates through the existing shared schema validation path. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` now proves the shipped gameplay encounter viewport can add an interaction marker, nudge it right/down, save the result, and persist the expected entity id/type/coordinates. This reduces the viewport gap to entity-level spatial editing; terrain/path/camera handle editing remains out of scope and stays marked partial in the requirement matrix. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.test.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx --testTimeout 30000` (`8` tests) and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web typecheck`.

- The `.gameview.json` editor now also supports route-level spatial editing. Selecting a viewport path exposes path controls in the inspector; creators can insert a new waypoint, nudge the entire selected route with inspector controls, use arrow keys on the viewport canvas to nudge the selected route, and save the updated point list through shared schema validation. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` now proves the shipped `critical-path` route can add a fifth waypoint, nudge left/up, save, and persist the expected point coordinates. The requirement matrix now marks viewport editing as entity-and-route capable while still partial for terrain/camera-handle editing. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.test.ts tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx --testTimeout 30000` (`9` tests) and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web typecheck`.

- The `.gameview.json` editor now supports camera-handle spatial editing. Shared `GameViewportDocument.cameraPlan` contract/schema entries accept optional `x`, `y`, `targetX`, and `targetY`; the shipped gameplay encounter viewport includes camera coordinates; the viewport renders camera handles and target lines; creators can add a new camera handle, nudge it with inspector controls, nudge it with arrow keys, and save coordinates through shared schema validation. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` now proves a new `camera-handle-3` can be added, moved, saved, and persisted with handle/target coordinates. The requirement matrix now marks viewport editing as entity, route, and camera-handle capable while leaving full terrain editing partial. - The `.gameview.json` editor now supports rectangular terrain-zone spatial editing. Shared `GameViewportDocument` contracts and `gameViewportDocumentSchema` include `terrainZones` with position, size, layer, traversal, cover, mood, and notes fields; the shipped gameplay encounter viewport now includes upper-cover and lower-stealth terrain zones; the viewport renders terrain-zone overlays; creators can add a new terrain zone, select it, nudge it with inspector controls, nudge it with arrow keys, and save its coordinates through shared schema validation. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` proves a new rectangular terrain zone can be added, moved, saved, and persisted with position/size/type fields. - The `.gameview.json` editor now supports terrain height-sculpt and mesh-deformation patches as first-class editable game-world data instead of only flat terrain annotations. Shared `GameViewportDocument` contracts and `gameViewportDocumentSchema` include `terrainSculptPatches` with sculpt centers, radii, height values, falloff, sample points, mesh intent, traversal impact, and notes; the shipped gameplay encounter viewport includes a Molten Ridge Sculpt for sightline, cover-value, and collision-readability planning. `GameStudioDocumentEditor` renders sculpt contours, sample labels, and a derived 5x5 per-vertex mesh-deformation preview from brush radius, height, and falloff data; exposes Add Sculpt Patch; supports inspector/arrow-key nudging; supports Raise/Lower Height controls; supports Add Brush Sample plus Broaden/Tighten Brush radius tuning; and saves shifted sample coordinates, brush radii, and adjusted height values through shared schema validation. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` now proves `terrain-sculpt-2` can be added, raised, expanded to four brush samples, widened, moved, rendered with 25 mesh vertices carrying derived height data, saved, and persisted with mesh-deformation sample/radius data. The requirement matrix now treats per-vertex terrain deformation preview as implemented for artifact editing, with WebGL and native engine runtime export covered in later entries. - The daemon playtest simulator now includes a deterministic terrain collision/deformation pass for `.gameview.json` artifacts. `apps/daemon/src/game-playtest-simulation.ts` samples sculpted terrain height at traversal route waypoints and segment midpoints, computes max simulated terrain height, max route height delta, steep-slope sample counts, and affected route ids, then emits terrain-specific metrics and findings for missing route collision samples, slope spikes, and missing collision/traversal intent. `apps/daemon/tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts` now proves a raised ridge sculpt on a critical traversal route produces `terrain-sculpt-patches`, `terrain-collision-samples`, `terrain-max-height`, and `terrain-steep-slope-samples` metrics plus a `terrain-deformation-slope-risk` finding while honoring explicit collision/traversal intent notes. The requirement matrix now treats deterministic route-sampled terrain collision/deformation simulation as implemented, with runtime exports covered below. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts --testTimeout 45000 --hookTimeout 45000` (`5` tests) and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon typecheck`.

- Gameview artifacts now have a generated WebGL runtime export instead of only static viewport documents and deterministic simulators. Shared contracts in `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` define game-engine runtime requests, responses, and `game_engine_runtime` SSE payloads; `apps/daemon/src/game-engine-runtime.ts` validates `.gameview.json` artifacts, emits a canvas-based WebGL HTML runtime with `window.__AGDS_GAMEVIEW_RUNTIME__`, `terrainHeightAt`, `terrainCollisionAt`, `advanceWorldTick`, weather/faction/dynamic-event state, terrain collider counts, and runtime hook metadata; and `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `POST /api/projects/:id/gameview-runtime` plus `/api/game-deliverables/:id/gameview-runtime`, with optional HTML artifact writing and project-event broadcast. `apps/daemon/tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts` proves the route generates `engine-runtime-webgl.html`, includes the WebGL canvas/runtime hooks, reports terrain/world counts, and emits `game_engine_runtime`. The requirement matrix treats WebGL gameview runtime export as implemented, with native engine package export added in the next entry.

- Gameview runtime export now also generates native Unity, Godot, and Unreal source packages from `.gameview.json` artifacts. Shared contracts add requested engines, generated file records, package write settings, and written file lists; `apps/daemon/src/game-engine-runtime.ts` can emit Unity `AGDSGameViewRuntime.cs`, Godot `agds_gameview_runtime.gd`, Unreal `AGDSGameViewRuntimeComponent.h/.cpp`, and an engine-export README with terrain height, terrain collision, dynamic event, weather, and faction-control hooks; and `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` can persist those generated packages under a project export directory and include written file paths in `game_engine_runtime` SSE. `apps/daemon/tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts` proves a native package request writes all five export files, exposes Unity/Godot/Unreal runtime hooks, preserves terrain/world counts, and broadcasts the generated package metadata. The requirement matrix now treats native engine handoff exporters as implemented for Unity, Godot, and Unreal. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts --testTimeout 45000 --hookTimeout 45000` (`7` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts typecheck`, and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon typecheck`.

- Project-scoped gameplay telemetry now has real daemon ingestion, production-design insight analysis, a typed SDK client, and a Production-surface telemetry board instead of only planning-language support. Shared contracts in `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` define telemetry event inputs, persisted records, summaries, insight categories, heatmap cells, responses, and SSE payloads. `apps/daemon/src/game-telemetry.ts` normalizes event batches, persists them as hidden project JSONL under `.agds/game-telemetry/events.jsonl`, reads recent events, summarizes counts by type/scene/session, and builds frustration/progression/accessibility/combat/economy/heatmap insights. `packages/contracts/src/game-telemetry-sdk.ts` provides a dependency-free telemetry client that queues contextual game events, posts them to the project ingestion route, preserves failed batches for retry, and is exported as `@ai-game-design-studio/contracts/game-telemetry-sdk`; `packages/contracts/tests/game-telemetry-sdk.test.ts` and `package-runtime.test.ts` prove SDK behavior and package-export importability. `apps/web/src/components/GameTelemetryBoard.tsx` renders insight counts, risk recommendations, and heatmap cells when the creator switches to the Production studio surface, and `apps/web/tests/components/GameTelemetryBoard.test.tsx` proves it stays idle until visible and renders telemetry data from `/game-telemetry/insights`. `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `GET/POST /api/projects/:id/game-telemetry`, `GET /api/projects/:id/game-telemetry/insights`, and matching `/api/game-deliverables` routes, updates the project timestamp on ingest, and emits `game_telemetry` SSE notifications for collaborating studio surfaces. `apps/daemon/tests/game-telemetry-routes.test.ts` proves event ingestion, persistence, summary generation, listing, insight/heatmap generation, validation rejection for missing event types, and project-event broadcast. The requirement matrix now treats real project-scoped ingestion, insight analysis, typed SDK support, telemetry board UI, and automatic balance-loop tuning as implemented. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts build`, `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts exec vitest run tests/game-telemetry-sdk.test.ts tests/package-runtime.test.ts --testTimeout 30000` (`5` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/components/GameTelemetryBoard.test.tsx --testTimeout 30000` (`1` test), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/web typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-telemetry-routes.test.ts tests/game-playtest-simulation-routes.test.ts tests/game-world-simulation-routes.test.ts tests/game-autonomous-iteration-routes.test.ts tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts tests/public-docs.test.ts tests/spec-docs.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`14` tests), `pnpm guard`, and `git diff --check`.

- Project-scoped runtime player-bot checks now exist for playable HTML game artifacts instead of only `.gameview.json` planning artifacts. - World-simulation, autonomous-iteration, balance, and studio-agent orchestration loops now have a scheduler-friendly offline runner for external timers. `apps/daemon/src/game-studio-scheduler.ts` opens the AGDS data directory directly, reads the project database and project files without starting the HTTP daemon, checks due/disabled loop state, runs due world-simulation, autonomous-iteration, `balance-loop`, and `studio-orchestration` passes, persists `lastState` / `lastActionCount` / `lastAdjustmentCount` / `lastAppliedCount` / `lastWorkOrderCount` / `lastHandoffCount` / `nextRunAt`, and supports `--force` semantics for explicit timer overrides. `apps/daemon/src/cli.ts` exposes `agds studio-scheduler run --project <id> [--task all] [--force] [--data-dir <path>]` with copy-ready launchd/systemd/cron/Windows Task Scheduler wording and visible `balance-loop` plus `studio-orchestration` tasks. `apps/daemon/tests/game-studio-scheduler.test.ts` proves due loops run directly from an isolated data directory with no server, future loops skip until due, and forced external passes run anyway; `apps/daemon/tests/cli-help.test.ts` proves the command is visible in root and subcommand help. - Automatic balance-loop tuning now exists as a concrete daemon subsystem instead of a telemetry-roadmap gap. Shared contracts in `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` define balance-loop requests, persisted loop config, adjustment records, responses, run responses, and SSE payloads. `apps/daemon/src/game-balance-loop.ts` converts telemetry insights, deterministic playtest findings, and high-risk game-system specs into prioritized tuning changes for combat readability, incoming damage, economy sink pressure, objective hint timing, checkpoint spacing, HUD/accessibility scale, recovery resources, route breadcrumbs, and session/reward cadence; it can keep those adjustments as reviewable recommendations or mark/apply them to `.systems.json` tuning values. `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `POST /api/projects/:id/balance-loop`, `GET/PUT/POST /api/projects/:id/balance-loop/loop`, mirrors the routes under `/api/game-deliverables`, starts daemon-local timers, reschedules persisted loops at daemon startup, clears timers on shutdown, and emits `game_balance_loop` / `game_balance_loop_config` project events. `apps/daemon/tests/game-balance-loop-routes.test.ts` proves telemetry plus playtest signals produce applied combat/economy tuning changes in a game-system document and that scheduled loop config/run metadata persists. - Studio document source editing now embeds collaborator cursors inside the JSON editor rather than only showing a remote-cursor strip above the workspace. `apps/web/src/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.tsx` maps `studio_presence` cursor metadata with `selectionKind: "cursor"` onto line-positioned source-editor markers, while retaining operation-level merge saves through the studio document operation route. `apps/web/src/index.css` gives the editor a stable overlay gutter that does not resize the source text area. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` proves a remote systems designer cursor renders both in the document cursor strip and inside the JSON source editor as `studio-doc-source-collab-cursors`; `apps/web/tests/components/FileWorkspace.test.tsx` keeps coverage for routing only matching active-file presence into the editor. - Studio document operation merging now supports bounded character-level source edits instead of only top-level JSON replacement/array operations. `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` adds the `text-splice` operation type; `apps/daemon/src/studio-document-operations.ts` validates bounded insert/delete ranges, applies text splices to the raw JSON source in deterministic lamport order, rejects splices that leave invalid JSON, then runs the normal game-studio document validation through the project write path. `apps/web/src/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.tsx` computes a minimal source diff between the persisted JSON and the next validated JSON and submits it as a `text-splice` operation before falling back to whole-file writes. `apps/daemon/tests/studio-document-operations-routes.test.ts` proves a character-level title replacement applies through the operation log, and `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` proves browser source edits submit `text-splice` operations. - Studio document source editing now live-merges valid JSON edits through debounced text-splice operations instead of waiting for the Save button. `apps/web/src/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.tsx` tracks source-editor-originated draft revisions, skips invalid in-progress JSON, submits a bounded `text-splice` operation after a short debounce, avoids overwriting newer local typing when an older merge returns, updates the persisted source after successful merges, and leaves spatial editor changes on the explicit save path. `apps/web/tests/components/GameStudioDocumentEditor.render.test.tsx` proves a valid source title edit calls the studio document operation route without pressing Save, while the existing spatial-edit save test stays on whole-file save fallback. - Studio document source editing now streams invalid in-progress JSON drafts through a separate source-draft operation channel while preserving canonical file validity. Shared contracts add `ProjectStudioDocumentDraftOperationResponse` and `studio_document_draft_operations` SSE payloads; `apps/daemon/src/studio-document-operations.ts` persists draft content and draft operation logs under `.agds/studio-document-drafts`, applies bounded text splices without JSON parsing, and keeps canonical project files untouched until valid operations land; `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `/studio-document-draft-operations` for projects and game deliverables. - Studio-agent orchestration now exists as a deterministic daemon subsystem rather than only prompt-level handoffs, and selected work orders can launch real role-scoped child-agent runs. Shared contracts in `packages/contracts/src/api/projects.ts` define orchestration requests, execution requests, loop config, file signals, work orders, handoff plans, debate plans, execution records, responses, run responses, and SSE payloads. `apps/daemon/src/game-studio-orchestration.ts` selects the relevant studio roles from project file signals and focus text, always includes direction/technical/accessibility/production coverage, builds ready or blocked work orders, attaches cross-discipline handoffs, schedules debate checkpoints, and renders role-specific execution prompts. `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` exposes `POST /api/projects/:id/studio-orchestration`, `POST /api/projects/:id/studio-orchestration/execute`, `GET/PUT/POST /api/projects/:id/studio-orchestration/loop`, mirrors the same routes under `/api/game-deliverables`, starts daemon-local timers, reschedules persisted loops at daemon startup, clears timers on shutdown, and emits `game_studio_orchestration`, `game_studio_orchestration_execution`, and `game_studio_orchestration_loop` project events. `apps/daemon/src/game-studio-scheduler.ts` and `apps/daemon/src/cli.ts` add the offline `studio-orchestration` scheduler task. The requirement matrix treats on-demand child-agent execution as implemented, with daemon-local scheduled execution/reconciliation added in the follow-up entry below. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-studio-orchestration-routes.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`3` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-studio-scheduler.test.ts tests/cli-help.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`4` tests), and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts typecheck`.

- Studio-agent orchestration loops can now opt into scheduled child-agent execution and dependency reconciliation instead of only planning work orders. Shared contracts add loop `autoExecute`, execution settings, last execution/run ids, and `GameStudioOrchestrationReconciliation`; `apps/daemon/src/game-studio-orchestration.ts` normalizes those loop fields, builds execution requests from loop config, and reconciles executed work orders into resolved dependency counts, follow-up-ready work orders, blocked work orders, run ids, and a summary. `apps/daemon/src/server.ts` reuses the same child-agent runner for `/studio-orchestration/execute` and `/studio-orchestration/loop/run`, persists execution/reconciliation metadata into loop config, emits `game_studio_orchestration_execution` and loop SSE metadata, and returns `executions` plus `reconciliation` from loop runs. `apps/daemon/tests/game-studio-orchestration-routes.test.ts` now proves a configured loop with `autoExecute: true` starts two fake child-agent runs, waits for completion, records run ids and executed work-order ids, emits execution and loop events with the reconciliation summary, and captures role-specific Game Design Studio prompts. The requirement matrix treats daemon-local scheduled role execution as implemented. Validation passed with `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon exec vitest run -c vitest.config.ts tests/game-studio-orchestration-routes.test.ts --testTimeout 30000 --hookTimeout 30000` (`4` tests), `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/contracts typecheck`, and `pnpm --filter @ai-game-design-studio/daemon typecheck`.

- Offline studio-agent scheduler passes now spawn configured role-scoped child-agent work orders directly from the project directory when the `studio-orchestration` loop has `autoExecute: true`. `apps/daemon/src/game-studio-scheduler.ts` resolves the loop's configured adapter or saved app-config agent, builds the same role-specific Game Design Studio work-order prompts used by the daemon route, spawns stdin/argv CLI adapters with `AGDS_OFFLINE_SCHEDULER`, `AGDS_PROJECT_ID`, `AGDS_PROJECT_DIR`, and `AGDS_DATA_DIR`, waits with a bounded timeout, persists execution counts/run ids/reconciliation summaries into the loop config, and returns scheduler result metadata for external timers. `apps/daemon/tests/game-studio-scheduler.test.ts` proves two fake OpenCode work orders execute without the HTTP daemon, capture Game Design Studio prompts, and persist reconciliation metadata. The requirement matrix now treats multi-agent scheduler execution as covered while keeping non-completion findings focused on native-language review and intentional compatibility aliases.

- Legacy compatibility aliases now have an explicit manifest instead of being tracked only as loose audit exceptions. `docs/game-design-compatibility-manifest.md` defines the only allowed legacy surfaces, their canonical AGDS/game-native replacements, why each alias remains, and the guard or test coverage that keeps it from becoming active product behavior. `apps/daemon/tests/compatibility-manifest.test.ts` proves the manifest names the guard-enforced boundaries, bans aliases from active prompts, visible UI copy, first-class skill folders, and first-class catalogs, and stays linked from both the audit and requirement matrix. The requirement matrix now treats compatibility aliases as governed migration/protocol constraints while leaving native-language review and broad final-state claims as the remaining non-completion findings.

The required `pnpm i18n:fallback-audit` command now protects the clean `0` exact fallback / `0` mixed-English phrase state instead of merely reporting it.
- Locale dictionary coverage is now explicit in the focused locale suite instead of relying only on TypeScript shape checks. `apps/web/tests/i18n/locales.test.ts` statically imports every locale dictionary, proves every non-English source file declares the same key set as English instead of inheriting English source copy, rejects mixed-English external-MCP connectivity/OAuth status fragments plus reviewed project-action/external-MCP helper fragments and reviewed artifact inspection/refresh-history helper fragments, and now runs the full locale suite as `55` tests on Node 24.
- Mobile workspace chrome now keeps the canonical `AI Game Design Studio` identity visible instead of collapsing to icon-only chrome below 520px. `apps/web/src/index.css` constrains the mobile `.studio-chrome-name` with ellipsis rather than `display: none`, and the full required Playwright studio-smoke shard passed with `23` browser tests across first-screen, studio-document, template-render, collaboration, presence, and runtime player-bot coverage.
- The dedicated requirement-matrix test now mirrors the completion verifier's concrete-evidence standard. `apps/daemon/tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts` rejects prose-only matrix rows, requires every row to cite concrete files/commands/tests/artifact paths, and proves each row includes at least one cited repository path that exists.
- The completion verifier now inspects actual game-studio surface inventory instead of trusting docs alone. `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` checks all required studio agent roles, collaboration handoffs, debate checkpoints, required game skills, top-level templates, named image/video game-media prompt families, visible game art bibles, core studio UI components, daemon game-system modules, game-system route fragments, and all 20 required game entity types across contracts plus daemon DB tables/indexes; `apps/daemon/tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts` now runs that verifier and asserts the agent, surface, media-prompt, and schema inventory reports are present.
- Game design tokens are now first-class shared contract data instead of prose-only guidance. `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` exports the 22 canonical visual, motion, and audio token families and validates them in `gameDesignTokensSchema`; `templates/combat-camera.systems.json` includes every family in a concrete game-system spec; `packages/contracts/tests/game-studio.test.ts` proves the rendered system prompt teaches every token family and keeps early camel-case specs readable; and `pnpm completion:audit` verifies the token inventory across contracts, prompt rules, and the systems template.
- Game-design evaluation coverage is now verifier-enforced instead of only unit-tested. `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` checks the 38 studio-grade axes in `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts`, verifies each has pattern coverage, and proves `apps/daemon/src/lint-artifact.ts` uses the shared evaluator for thin coverage, accessibility, and production feasibility feedback; `apps/daemon/tests/requirement-matrix.test.ts` asserts the evaluation inventory report.
- Game-design onboarding is now verifier-enforced instead of relying on visible labels alone. `apps/web/src/components/NewProjectPanel.tsx` captures the required game-brief metadata including genre, player mode, platform, 2D/3D, design focus, session, audience, emotional goal, monetization, art style, and inspirations; `apps/web/tests/components/NewProjectPanel.test.tsx` proves the create payload persists those fields; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` checks all 12 required onboarding prompts while blocking retired app/website brief copy.
- Community/modding systems now have dedicated artifacts instead of relying on adjacent multiplayer/live-ops guidance. `skills/game-community-modding/SKILL.md` covers UGC pipelines, creator ecosystems, moderation, creator rewards, compatibility rules, and telemetry; `craft/community-modding.md` defines modding scope ladders, community health signals, anti-patterns, and production gates; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `community_modding_ecosystem` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, memory schema, and evaluator axis.
- AI difficulty-director systems now have dedicated artifacts instead of only behavior-tree examples. `skills/game-difficulty-director/SKILL.md` covers intensity curves, adaptive enemy spawns, resource relief, safe-room pacing, skill signals, assist options, and telemetry; `craft/game-difficulty-director.md` defines observed signals, adjustment levers, trust rules, anti-patterns, and director telemetry; `templates/enemy-captain.btree.json` keeps concrete dynamic-difficulty rules; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `difficulty_director_adaptation` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, memory schema, behavior-tree template, and evaluator axis.
- Companion/party systems now have dedicated artifacts instead of relying on narrative and RPG adjacency. `skills/game-companion-party-systems/SKILL.md` covers companion identity, relationship progression, loyalty, betrayal, squad commands, party composition, tactical synergy, narrative reactivity, accessibility, and production budgets; `craft/companion-party-systems.md` defines party promise, relationship model, tactical model, AI trust rules, anti-patterns, and production budgeting; `templates/narrative-tree.html` keeps concrete companion reaction state; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `companion_party_systems` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, memory schema, narrative template, and evaluator axis.
- Dungeon/raid architecture now has dedicated artifacts instead of relying on adjacent level, boss, encounter, quest, and multiplayer guidance. `skills/game-dungeon-raid-architecture/SKILL.md` covers dungeon progression, raid wings, encounter sequencing, checkpoint logic, puzzle integration, boss pacing, reward escalation, role coordination, wipe recovery, telemetry, accessibility, and production budgets; `craft/dungeon-raid-architecture.md` defines run promise, macro path, raid structure, reward ladder, pacing model, checkpoint rules, anti-patterns, and production budgeting; `templates/roguelike-dungeon-crawler.html` and `templates/live-ops-calendar.html` keep concrete dungeon/raid surfaces; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `dungeon_raid_architecture` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, memory schema, templates, and evaluator axis.
- Narrative simulation now has dedicated artifacts instead of relying on narrative-branching adjacency. `skills/game-narrative-simulation/SKILL.md` covers reactive dialogue, morality systems, dynamic faction reactions, emergent storytelling, procedural narrative fragments, companion consequences, continuity ledgers, branch merge rules, and production budgets; `craft/narrative-simulation.md` defines state models, continuity ledgers, reactive dialogue rules, procedural narrative grammar, contradiction checks, emotional consequences, and telemetry; `templates/narrative-tree.html` keeps concrete branch-state variables; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `narrative_simulation_continuity` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, memory schema, narrative template, and evaluator axis.
- Adaptive design scaling now has dedicated artifacts instead of relying on feasibility prompt metadata. `skills/game-adaptive-design-scaling/SKILL.md` covers solo, indie, AA, and AAA variants, core-fantasy preservation, cutline matrices, content budgets, production gates, launch models, monetization fit, accessibility, telemetry, QA, and maintenance burden; `craft/adaptive-design-scaling.md` defines fantasy lock, scale ladder, system triage, content budget, production rules, anti-patterns, and stop/go gates; `templates/combat-camera.systems.json` keeps concrete scale variants; `packages/contracts/src/game-studio.ts` adds the `adaptive_design_scaling` evaluator axis; and `scripts/game-design-completion-audit.ts` verifies the skill, craft guide, feasibility/scaling memory schema, systems template, prompt memory ids, and evaluator axis.
- The Greybox Pro-module open-core boundary now has a signed metadata verifier without shipping proprietary bundle logic. `packages/contracts/src/api/pro-modules.ts` defines the `.gbpro` envelope, Ed25519 signature record, sanitized mount descriptors for skills, game art bibles, and engine targets, and `VerifiedProModuleManifest`; `apps/daemon/src/pro-module-loader.ts` scans `<project>/pro-modules/*.gbpro`, rejects unsigned, tampered, oversized, unsafe-path, and untrusted-key bundles, and returns only manifest metadata plus payload digest/signature key. `apps/daemon/tests/pro-module-loader.test.ts` proves signed bundles load, proprietary payload bytes are not returned, unsigned/tampered/unsafe bundles fail closed, and mixed project folders load only trusted manifests. Closed-core decryption, license-key fetch, and mount activation remain intentionally blocked behind signing-key custody and founder approval.
- Latest verification refresh passed under Node 24 after locale fallback gate hardening: `pnpm residual:language-audit` scanned `1200` source files and reviewed `536` classified matches with `0` undocumented matches while skipping generated Playwright `.agds-data` runtime folders, `pnpm i18n:fallback-audit` reported `0` exact English fallbacks and `0` scoped mixed-English phrase values across every non-English locale, focused i18n fallback audit tests passed including the report assertion and forced-failure fixture, full `pnpm typecheck` passed across the workspace, `pnpm guard` passed, `pnpm completion:audit` passed while still preserving the open final-review findings, and `git diff --check` passed.

Earlier pnpm commands emitted the same environment warning because the ambient shell used Node v25.9.0 while package engines request Node `~24`. Node 24.15.0 is now installed via nvm for verification runs; Node 24 commands above did not emit that engine warning.

## Completion Rule

Do not mark the transformation complete until:

- `pnpm guard` passes with no unexpected game-language violations.
- `pnpm completion:audit` passes and confirms the prompt-to-artifact checklist, required command set, 100-row matrix, compatibility manifest linkage, and open findings are still present.
- Focused tests for schema, prompt templates, NewProjectPanel, GameStudioDocumentEditor, and provenance/clipboard behavior pass.
- Package-level tests for daemon, web, contracts, tools-dev, and tools-pack pass.
- Playwright E2E shards for first-screen responsiveness, studio documents, game-template rendering, collaboration, presence, and runtime player-bot coverage pass, including desktop/mobile screenshot checks.
- Local browser smoke against `pnpm tools-dev run web --namespace agds-smoke --daemon-port 17645 --web-port 17646` verifies project creation, game-art-bible selection, game memory persistence, studio document rendering, playable HTML preview, and no legacy visible copy.
- Contracts, daemon, and web typechecks pass.
- `pnpm residual:language-audit` passes, and all remaining matches are either removed or documented as technical compatibility, negative guardrail/test language, third-party/protocol references, generated documentation assets, or historical audit records.
- The known gaps above are resolved or explicitly accepted as compatibility constraints by maintainers, with any retained alias listed in `docs/game-design-compatibility-manifest.md`.
